        "text": "# Release 0.83\n\n:material-calendar: 2026-11-06 · :material-list-status: [Full Changelog](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/releases)\n\n## ALE OmniSwitch vAOS\n\nThe new [`ale_vaos`](../manual/kinds/ale_vaos.md) kind adds support for Alcatel-Lucent Enterprise OmniSwitch virtual AOS 8.x as a vrnetlab-backed QEMU VM:\n\n```yaml\ntopology:\n  nodes:\n    os1:\n      kind: ale_vaos\n      image: vrnetlab/ale_vaos:8.11.162.R01\n      startup-config: os1.cfg\n```\n\nData interfaces `eth1` through `eth10` map to vAOS ports `1/1/1` through `1/1/10`, with `eth11` and `eth12` mapping to `1/1/29A` and `1/1/30A`. The launcher applies startup configuration over the serial console, configures the EMP management interface and saves the configuration. `containerlab save` writes the output of `show configuration snapshot` back to the node's startup-config file.\n\nSee the [ALE OmniSwitch vAOS kind documentation](../manual/kinds/ale_vaos.md) for image acquisition, management access and configuration options.\n\nDone in #3556 thanks to @Mathias-gt\n\n## Packet capture with `containerlab capture`\n\nThe new [`capture`](../cmd/capture.md) command records packets from one or more lab link interfaces into a single pcapng file, or streams them live into Wireshark. It works with Docker, Podman and c9s, without installing capture tools in the nodes:\n\n```bash\n# watch both ends of a link live in Wireshark\ncontainerlab capture --name srl \\\n  --target srl1:e1-1 --target srl2:e1-1 -w - | wireshark -k -i -\n\n# capture two interfaces for 30 seconds\ncontainerlab capture -t srl.clab.yml \\\n  --target srl1:e1-1,e1-2 --duration 30s -w srl.pcapng\n\n# list capturable interfaces and their peers\ncontainerlab capture --name srl --list\n```\n\nInterfaces can be selected by their name or topology alias, and every packet is associated with its `<node>:<interface>` name in the capture. Packets from all targets are merged in timestamp order. Captures can be bounded by duration or packet count, truncated to a snap length, and stopped cleanly with Ctrl+C while leaving a valid pcapng stream.\n\nWith Docker and Podman, containerlab captures through packet sockets in the node network namespaces; the command needs root, like `deploy`, but neither the host nor the node needs `tcpdump`. VLAN tags are preserved, interfaces that go down and back up remain captured, and a node restart is reattached with the missed interval reported as a gap. With c9s, the `clabwire` sidecar captures the packets, and long captures are chained without duplicating packets at the handover.\n\nThe [`inspect interfaces`](../cmd/inspect/interfaces.md) command now supports c9s as well, reporting lab interfaces with the same names, aliases and fields as the local runtimes.\n\nDone in #3542 thanks to @FloSch62\n\n## c9s runtime improvements\n\n/// warning | clabernetes 0.11 or newer required\nThe c9s runtime now requires clabernetes 0.11 or newer, which provides Node run state and the clabwire packet-capture contract. Containerlab checks the cluster's published Node schema before the first cluster operation and reports a clear error for older releases. Offline commands such as `validate`, `deploy --emit-crs` and file-based `graph` do not need cluster access.\n///\n\n### Controller-managed lifecycle and persistent state\n\n`stop`, `start` and `restart` now ask the clabernetes controllers to manage a node through its `Node` resource instead of editing Deployments. A stopped node stays stopped across deploy/apply operations and starts with its updated definition when explicitly started. Nodes sharing a pod through `network-mode: container:` follow their primary node's lifecycle.\n\nPersistent node volumes now survive a plain `destroy`, just as a local lab directory does. A subsequent deploy adopts the retained PersistentVolumeClaims and boots from the saved device state. `destroy --cleanup` removes the claims and managed namespace, while `deploy --reconfigure` and `redeploy --cleanup` also start from clean storage.\n\n### Docker-compatible inspection and events\n\nThe c9s runtime now presents nodes using Docker's state and event vocabulary, allowing clients to consume local and cluster labs in the same way:\n\n- `inspect` reports `created`, `running` and `exited` states, stable `<namespace>/<lab>/<node>` container IDs, the deployed topology file, and c9s-specific pod, container, Node UID, namespace, exposed-port and LoadBalancer fields in JSON output.\n- Container events use familiar actions such as `create`, `start`, `stop`, `die`, `destroy` and `health_status`, with the corresponding containerlab labels and node attributes.\n- Interface events and `inspect interfaces` expose the node's lab links, management interface and loopback under their Docker-equivalent names, while hiding clabernetes' internal pod interfaces.\n- Events can be scoped to one lab or namespace, and `inspect --all` and `destroy --all` honor the namespace override.\n- `graph --mermaid`, `--dot`, `--drawio` and HTML with `--offline` render directly from a topology file without connecting to a cluster.\n\nDone in #3539, #3541 and #3543, thanks to @FloSch62\n\n## Node logs and lab-runtime access\n\nThe new [`logs`](../cmd/logs.md) command displays or follows a node's log with Docker, Podman and c9s. With c9s it reads the log of the node's primary device container:\n\n```bash\ncontainerlab logs --name clos -n srl1 --tail 100 --follow --timestamps\n```\n\nContainerlab's Go API gained lab-runtime interfaces for locating nodes, opening interactive terminals, streaming logs, forwarding TCP ports, sampling CPU and memory usage, and capturing packets. The c9s implementation supports Kubernetes user impersonation per runtime or per call, applying the selected identity to REST requests, watches, logs, exec and port-forward connections. These APIs let services such as clab-api-server provide node access while Kubernetes RBAC continues to authorize each user.\n\nDone in #3540 thanks to @FloSch62\n\n## Selecting the management network of `mgmt-net` links\n\nWith [multiple management networks](../manual/network.md#multiple-management-networks), an extended [`mgmt-net` link](../manual/network.md#additional-connections-to-management-network) can now select its attachment network with the `network` key:\n\n```yaml\nname: multi\nmgmt:\n  - network: oob\n    ipv4-subnet: 172.31.10.0/24\n  - network: lab\n    ipv4-subnet: 172.31.20.0/24\n    bridge: br-lab\ntopology:\n  nodes:\n    r1:\n      kind: linux\n      image: alpine\n      mgmt-net: oob\n  links:\n    - type: mgmt-net\n      network: lab\n      endpoint:\n        node: r1\n        interface: eth1\n      host-interface: r1-eth1\n```\n\nWithout `network`, the link keeps attaching to the first management network, preserving existing behavior. Unknown networks and networks not using the bridge driver are rejected.\n\nDone in #3495 thanks to @FloSch62\n\n## MikroTik CHR improvements\n\n### Up to 64 data interfaces on AMD64\n\nThe [`mikrotik_chr`](../manual/kinds/mikrotik_chr.md) kind places data NICs behind PCI bridges on AMD64, raising the supported interface limit from `ether29` to `ether64` while preserving interface numbering and MAC addresses. ARM64 continues to support up to `ether30`.\n\nAt high port counts, the launcher verifies that RouterOS discovered every data NIC and re-plugs missing NICs before reporting the node healthy. It repeats the check after RouterOS reboots itself, so interfaces are recovered after an in-router reboot as well. Interface limits are checked during deployment, with errors naming the offending topology interface.\n\nDone in #3521 and #3568, thanks to @tomashoryl\n\n### Native management VRF and safer saves\n\nCHR management `ether1` now lives in a native `mgmt` VRF, while data interfaces remain in `main`. SSH, HTTP/HTTPS, API, Winbox, Telnet and SNMP are bound to the management VRF, so lab routes no longer take management replies away from `ether1`. LLDP, CDP and MNDP are disabled on management `ether1` by default while remaining enabled on data interfaces; a startup configuration can select another discovery policy.\n\nRepeated configuration saves now omit an invalid empty default keymat-provider entry emitted by recent RouterOS versions, preserve intentional discovery and service VRF settings, and filter launcher-owned management configuration more reliably.\n\n/// warning | Recreate nodes using the previous management setup\nThe native VRF setup requires a fresh node container. Export the user configuration before recreating an existing node that uses the previous policy-routing setup; the launcher does not migrate it in place.\n///\n\nDone in #3545, #3546 and #3547, thanks to @mischadiehm\n\n## SR Linux links survive chassis reboots\n\nA reboot issued from within an [SR Linux](../manual/kinds/srl.md#reboot) node, such as `tools platform chassis reboot`, now brings the node back with its links, saved configuration and management address intact.\n\nEach `nokia_srlinux` node uses an internal `<node-name>-netns` holder container for its network namespace. The holder is hidden from `inspect` unless `--all` is set and is removed with the node. SR Linux nodes now default to the `unless-stopped` restart policy, while an explicitly configured policy is preserved. Nodes with a user-defined `network-mode` keep the previous behavior.\n\nDone in #3567 thanks to @sacckth and @kaelemc\n\n## Per-node runtime deprecated\n\nThe node-level `runtime` parameter is deprecated and will be removed in a future release. Mixing runtimes within a lab is not supported because management networking and lab-wide operations use the lab-wide runtime. Setting `runtime` on a node, group, kind or defaults block still works but emits a deprecation notice.\n\nUse the global `--runtime` flag or `CLAB_RUNTIME` environment variable instead:\n\n```bash\ncontainerlab deploy -t mylab.clab.yml --runtime podman\n```\n\nDone in #3527 thanks to @FloSch62\n\n## Miscellaneous\n\n- `tools snapshot save` now snapshots the deployed nodes of a partially built lab instead of failing on nodes whose containers were not created. [#3537](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3537)\n- Ciena SAOS nodes now stage a topology-supplied license file—provided as a path, URL or embedded blob—into `/config/license` and expose it to the vrnetlab launcher through `SAOS_LICENSE_PATH`. [#3535](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3535)\n- SR-SIM finds its running instance using the full process command line, fixing discovery when FEX emulates SR OS on ARM hosts. [#3538](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3538)\n- `containerlab events` no longer misses interfaces created while a watcher starts. Failed netlink subscriptions are retried and re-established, with changes during the outage reconciled instead of silently ending interface reporting. [#3532](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3532)\n- FRR keeps `vtysh` available throughout installation of the configuration-save wrapper, closing a startup race that could prevent the initial configuration from loading. [#3555](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3555)\n- Cisco IOL now propagates a `mgmt-intf` override to the VM launcher when a non-default management interface is selected. [#3557](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3557)\n- Destroy and reconfigure operations reliably remove `vxlan-stitch` host interfaces by waiting for node deletion before host-link cleanup, including partial labs with no remaining containers. [#3571](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3571)\n- Open vSwitch bridges no longer accumulate stale auto-generated `clab-*` ports across deploy and redeploy operations when endpoint names require sanitized Linux interface names. Destroy also cleans up orphaned generated ports, and `ovs-vsctl` cleanup calls time out instead of hanging when `ovs-vswitchd` is unavailable. [#3573](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3573)\n- Empty node, kind and defaults definitions are handled correctly: nodes without a body inherit kind, group and configuration values, while empty kind or defaults blocks no longer cause panics. [#3518](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3518)\n- Docker and Podman resolve `network-mode: container:` targets correctly when a lab name contains the node name, fixing deployments of SR Linux nodes with their new network-namespace holders. [#3577](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3577)\n- The go-jose dependency was updated to 4.1.8. [#3569](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3569)\n- Documentation was clarified for [OcNOS image requirements](../manual/kinds/ipinfusion-ocnos.md), HwSKU-dependent [Dell SONiC interface names](../manual/kinds/dell_sonic.md), and [SR Linux platform licensing](../manual/kinds/srl.md#license). Reasoning
The latest release note (0.83) is released (dated 2026-11-06, "Release 0.83"). There's no unreleased rn file (like 0.84.md) apparently. The PR doesn't add a release note. Per repo conventions, small template fixes may go to Misc. But is a release note required? The release-note-review rule says to inspect changes under docs/rn/ — this PR has none. The repo convention (based on 0.83 Misc entries) suggests bug-fix PRs get a Misc bullet with a PR link. However, I shouldn't invent requirements. The manifest shows the head file list — check if there's a 0.84 rn file? Let me search docs/rn for the highest. The file list above stops at 0.83.md. Let me glob.

Now let me carefully assess the main technical concern: the `UpdateMgmtIntf` postdeploy path.

Scenario: A user has an existing IOL lab (deployed before this change, with NVRAM containing the old saved config, or even with NVRAM from a first boot where the user ran `wr`). After updating containerlab and redeploying:
- If the NVRAM has saved config (user saved), the running config includes no RA suppress; the new template line is never applied (template only used on first boot or when nvram is missing).
- `UpdateMgmtIntf` reapplies only mgmt addressing/routes.

But is the RA suppress something that would need convergence on redeploy? The PR intent: IOL with `ipv6 unicast-routing` sends RAs on mgmt network. On the containerlab docker mgmt bridge, RAs from IOL could cause IPv6 issues for other nodes/hosts on the mgmt network (e.g., Linux nodes doing SLAAC with `accept_ra` when forwarding enabled, or causing the mgmt bridge to be seen as having a router). Suppressing RAs on the IOL mgmt interface is the standard fix.

For an existing lab's NVRAM: on redeploy after upgrade, the old config persists and RAs keep being sent. Is this a real impact? The user could run `clab destroy -c`? Actually NVRAM persists across deploys; the only way to get the new line is to delete nvram or apply the command manually. Compare with the mgmt IP update path: containerlab explicitly re-applies mgmt addressing because the mgmt network may change between deploys (new network/subnet). The RA suppression isn't tied to network changes — it's static config. Still, adding `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` to the `UpdateMgmtIntf` idempotent command list would make existing labs converge on upgrade and matches the "idempotent and safe to re-run" comment. This is a legitimate P3 finding (consistency gap: fix doesn't reach existing labs).

Hmm, but is it truly intended that RA suppression also needs to be in update path? Consider: first boot applies template → config saved? Actually the bootstrap config is applied by IOL reading boot_config.txt at boot; whether it auto-saves to NVRAM is unclear — the containerlab docs say "When you save configuration in IOL to the NVRAM, the NVRAM configuration will override the startup configuration." So on subsequent boots without saved NVRAM, the boot config (with new line) is reapplied each boot. So the RA suppress line gets applied every boot for labs without saved NVRAM. The gap is only for labs with saved NVRAM (user-saved config). That's a modest but real scenario — users frequently `wr` in IOL labs.

Actually wait, there's also the case: first boot happened pre-upgrade (nvram exists but user never saved). Then boot_config.txt is regenerated with the new line but... does IOL re-apply boot config on non-first boots? Let's think: IOL reads config.txt at boot always? The iol.go PostDeploy comments: "Must update mgmt IP if not first boot, unless the node boots without a baseline config" — implying on non-first boot the baseline config is NOT reapplied (the node boots from its saved NVRAM config, and containerlab pushes updated mgmt IPs via console). Hmm, actually if nvram exists but wasn't saved by user... The nvram file is mounted; IOL persists running config to nvram automatically? IOL (Cisco IOL) saves config to nvram on `write memory` only. So non-saved nvram is empty; but then would the node re-apply boot_config? Actually the boot_config is mounted as /iol/config.txt which IOL applies as startup config at boot. Hmm, IOL uses config.txt as startup-config always? If so, on every boot the full boot config (including the new line) is applied, and UpdateMgmtIntf just fixes changed mgmt IPs. Then the "not first boot" mgmt update is needed because the running config from nvram... this is getting deep into IOL internals I can't verify from the snapshot.

Actually, from the docs: "Both types of startup configurations are only be applied on the first boot... When you save configuration in IOL to the NVRAM, the NVRAM configuration will override the startup configuration." This suggests boot config (default+partial) is applied on first boot; on later boots, if nvram has saved config it wins. Whether IOL re-applies config.txt when nvram is unsaved is ambiguous, but UpdateMgmtIntf exists precisely because mgmt addressing may change between deploys while the config persisted.

Either way, the convergence gap exists for labs with saved NVRAM configs: the RA suppress won't be applied, and since UpdateMgmtIntf deliberately re-applies idempotent mgmt commands, including it would be the root-cause-complete fix. I'll report it as P3.

Second potential issue: is `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` the right syntax and does it have side effects? In IOS, `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` suppresses RAs and also disables responding to RS on that interface. Side effect for the IOL node itself: none for its own address (it's statically configured, not SLAAC). For other nodes on the data-plane? This is only applied to the mgmt interface, not data interfaces. Fine.

Wait — one more consideration: does suppressing RAs on mgmt break anything that relied on RAs? E.g., other containers using the IOL as default IPv6 router on mgmt net? Unlikely, and the mgmt net has its own gateway semantics (docker). Fine.

Also check: template renders for L2 nodes too — fine.

Third: syntax check on the line — in IOS, `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` is a valid interface command (IOS 15+). OK.

Fourth: docs. Should the docs' list of "basic configuration" items mention RA suppression? The bullet list is a summary; arguably fine. But since the docs enumerate behavior and the cisco_iol.md is detailed, a note could be added. I'd call it optional P4 at most. Actually, is it user-visible behavior? Yes: IOL nodes no longer send RAs on the mgmt network — visible behavior change for users who relied on it. Release note guidance: repo convention (0.83 Misc) lists small changes. The PR adds no release note entry. Given the release-note-review rule and convention, I could flag missing release note as P4. Hmm. The rule only governs changes under docs/rn/ (mutability). It doesn't mandate adding one. The repo's convention appears to add misc bullets for such fixes. I think a P4 suggestion is reasonable: add a release-note bullet, since the change alters default node behavior.

Also: docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md line 152 list — could add. Minor.

Now, also check `UpdateMgmtIntf`'s mgmt_str: it does `no ipv6 address` then `ipv6 address ...`. If a user relocates mgmt interface, RA suppress on old interface? Not relevant.

Another angle: the template applies the suppress only on the mgmt interface. But data interfaces configured with IPv6 addresses (lines 37-39) also send RAs on data links — could that be an issue? On point-to-point lab links, RAs could cause other nodes to form default routes via IOL. Many other node kinds don't suppress. The PR scoped to mgmt — that's a deliberate scope; not a defect. Could note as coverage note, not a finding.

Now check tests: no test covers template rendering. One-line change; ponytail says trivial one-liners need no test. The iol_test.go exists but doesn't test the template. Not a finding.

Schema: no topology schema change; not needed.

Let me double-check the diff context: the added line is inside `interface {{ .MgmtIntf }}` block before `no shutdown`. Yes.

Also check the `boot_config.txt` regeneration: GenBootConfig is called every PostDeploy, writing boot_config.txt. On non-first boots, if IOL re-applies config.txt... it doesn't matter.

One more check: is there anywhere else the IOL template commands get validated, e.g., a CI test for cisco_iol that runs the deployment (`.github/workflows/cisco_iol-tests.yml`)? Let me look at it quickly to see whether the PR would be exercised. Not needed for findings but for coverage notes.

Also confirm there's no other place where the default config is described as verbatim (like a doc embedding the template). grep for "ipv6 unicast-routing" in docs.

Let me run a couple of greps.

Reasoning
The latest release note is 0.83, published. No unreleased note exists. Adding one is a convention thing; I'll keep it as P4 suggestion.

Now the main finding: convergence gap for existing labs via `UpdateMgmtIntf`. Let me carefully assess. On redeploy of an existing IOL lab (nvram present, config saved with `wr`), the running config lacks `ipv6 nd ra suppress all`. The `UpdateMgmtIntf` command string re-applies only: `no switchport` (l2), `ip address`, `no ipv6 address`, `ipv6 address`, and the two mgmt routes. The comment says "All IOS commands applied here are idempotent and safe to re-run." Adding `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` (idempotent) after `ipv6 address` would converge existing labs. This is the root-cause-completeness point from the ponytail rule ("grep every caller... fix the shared function once"). Severity: P3 — a real behavioral gap for pre-existing labs (RAs keep flowing, the very problem the PR fixes), but limited to labs with saved config.

Wait — actually consider the scenario more carefully. Suppose NVRAM exists but the user never saved. Then what does the node boot with? IOL boots applying /iol/config.txt (boot_config.txt) each boot? If the node applies boot_config.txt at every boot regardless of NVRAM save state, then the new line applies on every boot for non-saved labs, and the only gap is saved configs. The docs statement "Both types of startup configurations are only be applied on the first boot" suggests on later boots, only the user-saved NVRAM config (or the persisted default?) applies. Hmm, ambiguous. Actually IOL persists its running config to nvram on write memory only; at boot, IOL reads config.txt as the startup config? The UpdateMgmtIntf exists because on later boots, the mgmt network could have a different subnet, so containerlab pushes updated IPs. If config.txt were applied at every boot with freshly rendered mgmt IPs, UpdateMgmtIntf would be unnecessary. So evidently on non-first boots the node does NOT apply boot_config.txt; the NVRAM (possibly auto-saved by IOL, or empty) governs. Actually with IOL, NVRAM is a file that IOL itself writes when you `wr`. Hmm.

Regardless of the exact mechanism, the gap remains: for labs whose running config persists across the upgrade, the RA suppress line will not be applied by this PR's changes alone. The fix belongs in `UpdateMgmtIntf`'s idempotent command list (and that's also where the equivalent `no ipv6 address`/`ipv6 address` pair lives, suggesting the RA suppress belongs next to it). Solid P3.

Is there any risk that adding it to UpdateMgmtIntf would matter? It's idempotent. OK.

Another check: does the template apply RA suppression when the node boots with `bootstrap-config: none`? Then no baseline config at all — no mgmt addressing either; that's by design.

Coverage: I reviewed the template (head and base), iol.go (GenBootConfig, UpdateMgmtIntf, GenInterfaceConfig), iol_test.go, cisco_iol docs, release notes, workflow. I did not run tests (source-only review). The tests in tests/10-basic-cisco_iol — do they check the boot config content? Let me quickly check the robot tests for any assertion on config content that might need updating.

# Code Review: Cisco IOL mgmt IPv6 RA suppression

## Summary

The PR adds a single line — `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` — to the management interface block of the embedded Cisco IOL bootstrap template (`nodes/iol/iol.cfg.tmpl`). The template is embedded via `//go:embed` into `iol.go` and rendered by `GenBootConfig` on every deploy. The command itself is valid IOS interface configuration and is correctly placed inside the `interface {{ .MgmtIntf }}` block with matching indentation; it composes fine with the existing `vrf forwarding clab-mgmt`, static `ipv6 address`, and the L2 `no switchport` conditional. No schema changes are needed for this change.

The change is minimal and focused. The two findings below are about completeness of the fix (the same suppression is missing from the postdeploy management-update path used by existing labs) and about documentation of a user-visible behavior change.

| Priority | Count | Summary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P0 | 0 | — |
| P1 | 0 | — |
| P2 | 0 | — |
| P3 | 1 | Postdeploy `UpdateMgmtIntf` does not apply the new RA suppression, so existing labs never converge |
| P4 | 1 | User-visible behavior change is not reflected in release notes or the IOL kind documentation |

## Findings

### P3: Existing IOL labs never receive the RA suppression because `UpdateMgmtIntf` re-applies only addressing**Location:** [nodes/iol/iol.go, lines 719–732](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/9323d7eff382af82e90367ce52a25c4312b7e68e/nodes/iol/iol.go#L719-L732) (introduced gap stems from [nodes/iol/iol.cfg.tmpl line 30](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/9323d7eff382af82e90367ce52a25c4312b7e68e/nodes/iol/iol.cfg.tmpl#L30-L30))

**Problem:** The new `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` line is only part of the first-boot bootstrap template. The `PostDeploy` → `UpdateMgmtIntf` path exists precisely to push idempotent management commands to nodes whose configuration persists in NVRAM across deploys — its comment states "All IOS commands applied here are idempotent and safe to re-run", and it already re-sends `no ipv6 address` / `ipv6 address` for exactly this convergence purpose. It does not include the RA suppression command.

**Trigger:** Deploy an IOL lab with a pre-existing containerlab release, save the configuration to NVRAM (`write memory`, as the [cisco_iol.md docs](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/9323d7eff382af82e90367ce52a25c4312b7e68e/docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md#L169-L169) describe as the normal persistence flow), then upgrade containerlab and redeploy. The saved NVRAM configuration wins over the regenerated `boot_config.txt`, and `UpdateMgmtIntf` only re-pushes addressing and routes.

**Impact:** For all pre-existing labs, the node keeps emitting IPv6 Router Advertisements on the management network — the exact problem this PR intends to stop — with no error or indication. New labs are fixed; upgraded ones silently are not.

**Suggestion:** Append `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` to the `mgmt_str` command sequence in `UpdateMgmtIntf`, right after the `ipv6 address %s/%d` push (the suppression is interface-static and idempotent, matching the existing command list). This is a one-line change that makes the fix converge for existing labs at the shared root cause rather than only on first boot.

### P4: User-visible default behavior change is not documented in the release notes or IOL kind docs

**Location:** [nodes/iol/iol.cfg.tmpl, line 30](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/9323d7eff382af82e90367ce52a25c4312b7e68e/nodes/iol/iol.cfg.tmpl#L30-L30); missing counterparts: [docs/rn/](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/9323d7eff382af82e90367ce52a25c4312b7e68e/docs/rn/0.83.md) (latest is the published 0.83 note; no unreleased note exists) and [docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md, lines 152–158](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/9323d7eff382af82e90367ce52a25c4312b7e68e/docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md#L152-L158)

**Problem:** Suppression of Router Advertisements changes observable default behavior of every IOL node's management interface (the node no longer advertises itself as an IPv6 router on the management network), and it is not covered by the "basic configuration which configures the following" enumeration in `cisco_iol.md` nor by any release note. The repository convention (e.g., the `Miscellaneous` bullets in `docs/rn/0.83.md`) records such kind-level default changes in release notes.

**Trigger:** A user reading the IOL documentation or release notes cannot learn that RAs are now suppressed on the management interface; someone debugging SLAAC or RA-related behavior on their management network would not connect it to this default.

**Impact:** Low — purely informational, but the docs list is otherwise maintained against the template, and this is a deliberate default change.

**Suggestion:** Add one line to the bullet list in `docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md` (e.g., "Router Advertisements are suppressed on the management interface.") and, per the repo's convention, a one-line entry in the next unreleased release note's `Miscellaneous` section once it exists.

## Coverage notes

- The diff touches exactly one file, `nodes/iol/iol.cfg.tmpl`. Both the base and head versions were read in full; the added line is syntactically and positionally correct within the management interface block and renders identically for L2 and routed types.
- Traced the full template consumer path: `//go:embed iol.cfg.tmpl` → `GenBootConfig` → `boot_config.txt` (written on every `PostDeploy`), and the parallel postdeploy path `UpdateMgmtIntf`, which is the basis for the P3 finding. `GenInterfaceConfig` and interface mapping are unaffected.
- Checked `nodes/iol/iol_test.go` (covers `UpdateMgmtIntf` timeout and L2 detection; no template-content assertions exist) and the `tests/10-basic-cisco_iol` Robot suite (no assertions on `nd ra`, `boot_config`, or NVRAM content). A rendered-config assertion would be a possible follow-up but is not required for a one-line template change.
- No topology schema implications: this is an internal node-kind template change, so no `schemas/clab.schema.json` edit is expected.
- Deliberately out of scope of the PR, noted without a finding: data interfaces configured with IPv6 addresses (template lines 37–39) also send RAs on lab links; the PR scopes suppression to the management interface only, which looks intentional.
- This was a source-only review: no tests, builds, or deployments were executed, and no runtime verification of the `ipv6 nd ra suppress all` command against a specific IOL image was possible. Command validity is assessed from knowledge of standard IOS/IOS-XE interface configuration.
